### Supporting proof or source audit: part_b_a2_regularity_audit.md

# Narrow independent audit of the A2 collar and marked descent

19 November 2026, completed at17:40:13 UTC. GPT-6 Astra, OpenAI, agent `part_b_cycles`, independent reviewer. This is a targeted check of a concrete regularity risk, not a new theorem, a status change, or additional substantive research movement.

**Outcome: no gap found in the current collar/Beltrami construction or the fixed marked descent.** The construction does not assert that the moving hyperbolic/flat metric is globally analytic. Its complex structure is smooth across the seam; that is the object to which analytic parameter dependence is applied. No manuscript or bibliography edit is requested.

## Scope and original evidence

The current `sections/part_a2b_secondary.tex:32–113` was read in full, together with its first-variation use at lines123–215, `notes/part_c_a2b_renewed_audit.md`, and the earlier root precision notes. The parent subsequently narrowed the requested check to a possible failure of the collar construction or marked descent, because the energy signs, mixed Green identity, zero faces, and recession argument had already been checked. This note respects that boundary and does not claim a fresh audit of the global image proof.

The relevant original Mondello passages were read from `sources/mondello/Triang-submitted.tex:3494–3509,3535–3541,4078–4102`: Theorem5.4 and the fixed-cusp discussion in §5.6 supply the finite-stratum real-analytic diffeomorphism. The source explicitly distinguishes analytic grafting dependence from the C1,1 regularity of the grafted metric. Public identifiers are [arXiv0804.0605](https://arxiv.org/abs/0804.0605) and [DOI10.4171/JEMS/263](https://doi.org/10.4171/JEMS/263).

The original Ahlfors–Bers paper was independently reopened through its indexed [original PDF](https://www.cimat.mx/~mmoreno/teaching/fall10/AhlforsBers.pdf). Actual tool references are `turn443622104view0`, `turn916237710view0`, and `turn916237710view1`. Section5.7, Theorem10, printed pp402–403 gives differentiable parameter dependence; Theorem11, pp403–404 gives holomorphic dependence; the **corollary on printed p404** explicitly gives real-analytic dependence of the normalized disc solution on real parameters. The latter is the most direct formulation for the marked universal-cover step. OCR slips in displayed differential equations are not used to infer any additional theorem. No claim is made to have opened a new physical PDF file on disk during this audit.

## 1. The cutoff gives a valid fixed compactification

At a fixed positive parameter, let a=λ/λ₀ and use the reference collar ζ=x+iy, with x of period2πλ₀. Near the seam the coordinate change is `(x,y)↦(ax,y)`. Pulling back either metric gives the conformal class of `a²dx²+dy²`; the core's additional scalar factor is sec²y. Therefore both sides have exactly the same Beltrami coefficient `(a−1)/(a+1)`, including all spatial derivatives at the seam. The unmatched second derivative of the scalar density does not enter the complex structure.

For the flat-side cutoff in manuscript lines56–67,

`∂y y_s = 1+(a−1)(χ(y)+yχ′(y))`.

The parenthesized function is bounded: it vanishes near the seam, is1 sufficiently far out, and has compact transition. Thus a parameter neighborhood of a=1 makes this derivative uniformly positive. Periodicity is respected by the x-rescaling. Far out, ζ_s=aζ and λ_s=aλ₀, so the filled-disc coordinate `exp(iζ_s/λ_s)` is exactly `exp(iζ/λ₀)`. In particular the identification extends over the marked center and introduces no parameter-dependent singularity there. The remaining cusp charts are fixed.

The fixed smooth cutoffs need not be analytic in the spatial variable. Their parameter dependence is real-analytic in every finite C^k norm, which is the relevant assertion. Combining them with local analytic Fenchel–Nielsen representatives gives a smooth Beltrami coefficient depending real-analytically on the finite-dimensional parameter in C^k, hence in L∞, with norm uniformly below1 after shrinking the parameter neighborhood. This is enough for Ahlfors–Bers. It does not replace the piecewise metric by a globally analytic one.

## 2. Analytic normalized maps descend with the marking

Use the universal cover of the stable punctured reference surface. Its deck group is non-elementary, including the stable sphere and once-punctured torus cases. Lift the invariant coefficient and take the normalized **disc** solution. The p404 corollary gives real-analytic parameter dependence; equivalently one can use reflection and the plane theorem. Reflection is an available normalization, not an assumption that arbitrary plane-normalized solutions preserve the disc.

For each deck transformation γ, invariance makes the conjugate Γ_s(γ) a disc Möbius transformation. Its coefficients depend analytically on s without requiring boundary regularity of the normalized solution: take three fixed, distinct interior points z_j, and recover the unique Möbius transformation from the three pairs `W_s(z_j)↦W_s(γz_j)`. All six points depend analytically on s and remain distinct locally.

The marked conformal class is fixed by the already used infinite-grafting inverse. Consequently these representations are conjugate to the fixed marked representation. Choose three reference hyperbolic elements with distinct attracting fixed points. Their varying fixed points are simple roots of the corresponding Möbius fixed-point equations, hence are analytic functions of the real parameters. The unique disc Möbius map matching the three ordered fixed points gives an analytic local choice of conjugacy. It is the required conjugacy for the whole group, since such a marked conjugacy already exists and the three-point normalization determines it. A lift can be chosen locally near the reference lift; the possible inner-conjugacy ambiguity is discrete. Thus the normalized maps descend to the fixed Y with the prescribed marking.

Finally, a stable marked compactification has no nonidentity conformal automorphism isotopic to the identity relative to the marks. This removes ambiguity in the descended marked isomorphism. The argument uses fixed **marked** Teichmüller data; it would not follow merely from equality of unmarked moduli points.

## 3. Spatial regularity is sufficient for the first shape derivative

Ahlfors–Bers starts with dependence in its indicated Hölder/Sobolev Banach spaces. On every compact subchart of the lifted surface, the coefficients are smooth spatially and analytic in the parameter. Interior elliptic estimates for the Beltrami equation give locally uniform bounds in any prescribed finite spatial differentiability norm, for the solution and parameter derivatives. More explicitly, the real-analytic coefficient can be complexified locally in parameter, preserving a uniform ellipticity bound; the holomorphic solution has locally uniform higher spatial bounds by the same estimates. Cauchy estimates in parameter then give the corresponding analytic dependence in each lower finite spatial norm. Thus the bootstrap does not consist merely of a formal differentiation of an L∞ solution.

At a cap center the tail trivialization has zero Beltrami coefficient. The descended map is holomorphic there, so its derivative at the center is analytic in s by the Cauchy integral formula. The conformal radius, a positive analytic modulus of a nonzero derivative after the stated normalization, is real-analytic. Across the seam the descended maps are spatially smooth. The individual metric pieces remain analytic after their separate trivializations, with equal value and first spatial jet on the moving seam. This is sufficient to differentiate the matching conditions once in the parameter.

Specifically, at the reference seam their logarithmic densities have equal zeroth and first jets, and second normal derivatives1 on the core and0 on the cylinder. The first Eulerian variation is consequently continuous. Differentiating the matched first normal derivative along the seam of normal speed V gives the stated one-sided jump `h_y,+−h_y,−=V`. This step does not differentiate the whole metric twice across the seam, nor assume analytic dependence at a fixed point crossed by the seam. The existing Green calculation applies on the fixed base pieces with these traces.

## Audit record and limits

This was a hand proof audit with local file reads and one targeted original-source reopening. No symbolic calculation, finite enumeration, numerical experiment, source download, or mathematical program was run. The targeted web `find` call returned in a tool-reported0.8seconds; a separate high-resolution runtime was not instrumented. No raw external source body was written inside the deliverable project. Only the narrow audit note and one event were added. The conclusion is that the specific suspected regularity defect is absent under the manuscript's stated fixed, stable, marked hypotheses; it is not a new theorem or source-access closure.

## Original passages reread for this bounded check

- **Broe**, original saved author source `sources/broe/TateAbelianFourfold.tex:240–260,778–799`: Theorem 1.1 gives the finite-field Tate statement in codimension two with `Q_l` coefficients for every invertible prime; Corollary 1.2 and Corollary 8.2 give D over arbitrary fields. The paragraph at line 781 expressly fixes rational cycle coefficients for equality of equivalence relations. Corollary 8.1(i) is the finite-field rational=numerical input. Remark 1.3 distinguishes the two positivity formulations. Original identifier: [arXiv 2608.28651v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28651v1).
- **Ancona**, original saved author source `sources/ancona/fourfolds_2020.tex:369–417,438–461,490–530,570–589`: the conventions require invertibility of the realization prime; Conjecture 3.10 is positivity on numerical primitive classes; Proposition 3.11 and Corollary 3.12 distinguish the homological radical and semipositivity; Theorem 3.18 gives the positive-characteristic Hodge-type result; Corollary 3.19 transfers equality from a specialization; Theorem 3.20 asserts equality for infinitely many primes. The characteristic-zero Hodge–Riemann statement is separately discussed at lines 525–530. Original identifier: [arXiv 1806.03216v3](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.03216v3).

### Supporting proof or source audit: part_a567_toric_handoff_consistency.md

# Final handoff wording audit: A1 raw toric isotopy counterexample

Date: 2026-11-19, approximately18:11–18:14UTC. Agent: part_a567.
Scope: consistency of the current statement, proof wording, outcome-table generator and locators. No proof, numerical calculation, phase search, source retrieval or mathematical program was repeated.

## Main outcome

The three requested distinctions are consistent in the active theorem and report generator. No mathematical or definition-scope mismatch was found. One minor CSV status-polarity ambiguity is reported separately below; no requested file was edited.

The active fragment is sections/part_a567_toric_descent.tex, included by sections/part_a1.tex:170. It is therefore part of the current paper, not merely an unused draft.

| Statement ID | Current compiled number | Statement start page | Source locator |
|---|---|---:|---|
|A1-pant-third-jet|Lemma1.16|15|part_a567_toric_descent.tex:13–64|
|A1-four-point-plumbing-jet|Lemma1.17|16|part_a567_toric_descent.tex:65–168|
|A1-toric-nondescent|Theorem1.18|17|part_a567_toric_descent.tex:170–276|

These mappings agree with logs/RESEARCH_SA22_paper.aux:48–50, the current aggregate CSV's A1-toric-nondescent row, and the generated report's A.1 outcome row. The generator reads those actual auxiliary labels at code/root_reconcile_report.py:11–18 and resolves them in the outcome table at67–68.

## Requested wording checks

**Unevaluated analytic phase.** The fragment's opening lines9–11 call this an exact analytic counterexample and expressly distinguish its deterministic analytic rule from an evaluated numerical angle. Lines243–252 define N as the least positive n for which Delta(exp(i/n)) is nonzero, prove existence by the identity theorem, and explicitly assert no numerical value of N. The same qualification appears in the owned results row, live mathematical-scope record, and generator paragraph at code/root_reconcile_report.py:72. No finite enumeration, numerical welding certificate, or evaluated phase is claimed in these checked statements. The exact coefficient identity is not described as a numerical approximation (fragment:160–162).

**Two cuts in the same marked surface.** The theorem statement at172–177 names one four-labelled X and two isotopic cuts C,C_geo within it. The proof's unequal X and balanced X_G at255–264 are auxiliary comparison surfaces. The actual same-surface pair is then explicitly C and X's own geodesic C_geo at266–275. The generator's A.1 row at41 and explanatory paragraph at72 both say that the unequal seam and its own geodesic have different tensors in the same X. They do not substitute C on X and a curve on the distinct X_G as the counterexample pair. The fixed four external labels are retained; choosing a topological marking of that single X does not introduce a second surface.

**Separate geodesic convention.** Fragment:178–179 explicitly identifies replacing a representative by its geodesic before surgery as the different well-defined convention. The generator repeats that distinction at41 and72. The A.1 definition text at sections/part_a1.tex:10–16 separately identifies equal-length geodesic representatives and broader unequal-length toric compatibility. The older radius-pair noninjectivity example remains labelled as distinct actual cut objects in generator:42; it is not silently promoted into the same-X isotopy counterexample.

The exact manuscript-interpretation correction is still attached to inputs/RESEARCH_SA22_transcriptions/transcriptions/MPL_67_053-101_transcription.tex:939–963 (67/079–080). Those lines discuss possibly nongeodesic circles, their unique geodesics and the tentative reciprocal-rescaling heuristic, including the manuscript's own expressed doubt. The current correction row and labels map to that passage consistently.

## Minor metadata polarity ambiguity, reported without edits

checks/part_a567_results.csv:42 gives the statement in negative form, namely that raw canonical toric surgery is **not invariant**, while its status cell says **Refuted**. The current aggregate RESEARCH_SA22_results.csv:20 similarly places the proved counterexample statement next to Refuted.

The active theorem caption is unambiguous: **Refuted: raw toric surgery is invariant under isotopy in a fixed surface**. The outcome-table generator also attaches Refuted to the positive invariance/descent claim. Thus the CSV cell is understandable as the status of the manuscript claim, but a reader treating the status as applying literally to the adjacent negated statement could read it backwards. This is a metadata wording ambiguity, not a changed mathematical conclusion or an error in the phase or same-X argument.

The parent was alerted. A consistent resolution, if desired, is to clarify that the CSV status applies to the original positive claim, or to make that positive claim the row's status-bearing text while retaining the proved counterexample in its notes. No TeX, generator, CSV, aggregate or mathematical status was changed by this audit. It does not count as progress movement.

# Read-only audit of Refuted-row polarity

19 November2026. Reviewer: GPT-6 Astra Pro, OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode; agent `part_c_algebra`.

**Scope:** all159current aggregate CSVrows were inventoried; all28rows with status `Refuted` were compared with their active TeXcaptions and contributor CSVrows. No TeX, CSV, aggregate, proof or certificate was edited or rerun. This is reporting clarity, with no mathematical/status movement.

## Aggregate-level cause and sufficient repair

`code/assemble_records.py:89` unconditionally replaces contributor `statement` text with the active theorem body. Those bodies correctly assert the proved counterexample or corrected result, while `Refuted` classifies the original claim addressed by the result. Thus positive contributor rephrasings alone will not fix the aggregate.

A uniform prefix is sufficient;28handwritten positive-claim mappings are unnecessary:

> Proved refutation and corrected scope (active caption: …): [existing body]

Standard notes sentence:

> Refuted classifies the original manuscript claim addressed here; the statement field records its proved refutation and any corrected scope.

Use **active caption**, rather than claiming every caption literally states the positive original claim. For example, the A7-domain-counterexample and B1c captions describe the proved negative conclusion. This does not require a mathematical or TeXstatus change.

### Primary-source wording at handoff

# Primary-source wording check for B.1 at handoff

At19November2026,17:58–18:00UTC, the root reread the actual saved Broe original on printed pp1–2: Theorem1.1, Corollary1.2 and Remark1.3. The first gives the finite-field Tate statement; the second explicitly states numerical–homological equality for every abelian fourfold over an arbitrary field and every invertible prime; the remark distinguishes numerical primitive positivity from Grothendieck's homological formulation. This corroborates the already written deduction and its explicit preprint dependency. It is not an independent audit of the entire preprint proof.

The actual Ancona arXiv abstract was reopened as `turn333609143view1` and explicitly states the infinitely-many-primes conclusion. The concurrent Broe abstract-page open returned DisabledError in this web call; this does not erase the earlier acquired original PDF/LaTeX or the original passages just reread. A separate recent primary-source search returned the indexed arXiv record as `turn805877605academia9`, with the exact title, author and2026preprint scope. Broader returned results were not used to add an all-powers theorem or any new result to the manuscript.

The two calls took0.789seconds and1.268seconds respectively. Exact executed bodies and compact outcomes are retained in `code/root_b1_handoff_sources.js` and its output JSON. Complete returned source bodies are outside the package. There is no change to the statement, its proof, its status, or the outstanding original-reading gates; this check is not substantive review movement.

No open requirement changed status and no main question reduced to a strictly smaller gate. Pfluger1960, Lieberman1968 and Fujiwara1995 remain unread in the prescribed original mathematical passages.
